25. Illustrated examples of the analysis of stress criticality of the observed natural fractures in six of the vertical key wells (referred to as 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6) in the Khuff of the study area. (A) Mohr diagrams compared with the Mohr-Coulomb shear failure envelopes and polar plots (lower hemisphere equal area projection of lines perpendicular to fracture planes) for each of the wells 1 to 6. The polar plot is color coded according to the Coulomb failure function to show how close a particular fracture is to shear failure under the reservoir stress conditions. Shear slip requires the function to be greater than zero, which means the applied shear stress on the fracture is greater than needed for shear slip. (B) Tadpole plots of all the fractures versus total vertical depth TVD in ft for each of the wells 1 to 6. The fractures in the plots are color coded according to the stress status: white (in the polar plot and Mohr diagram) and red (in the tadpole plots): predicted critically stressed fractures, black: stable fractures, green: fractures perpendicular to the minimum stress and are potentially affected by dilation. Under reservoir conditions the fractures are not critically stressed. In order to reach stress criticality we have to assume zero cohesion, as illustrated in one of the key wells (well 6) or increase the pore pressure significantly. Reduction of the cohesion to zero in well 6 would only induce stress criticality in $5 . 5 \\%$ of the fractures in this well. Fracture aperture enhancement","quote":"Figure 25. Illustrated examples of the analysis of stress criticality of the observed natural fractures in six of the vertical key wells (referred to as 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6) in the Khuff of the study area. (A) Mohr diagrams compared with the Mohr-Coulomb shear failure envelopes and polar plots (lower hemisphere equal area projection of lines perpendicular to fracture planes) for each of the wells 1 to 6. The polar plot is color coded according to the Coulomb failure function to show how close a particular fracture is to shear failure under the reservoir stress conditions. Shear slip requires the function to be greater than zero, which means the applied shear stress on the fracture is greater than needed for shear slip. (B) Tadpole plots of all the fractures versus total vertical depth TVD in ft for each of the wells 1 to 6. The fractures in the plots are color coded according to the stress status: white (in the polar plot and Mohr diagram) and red (in the tadpole plots): predicted critically stressed fractures, black: stable fractures, green: fractures perpendicular to the minimum stress and are potentially affected by dilation. Under reservoir conditions the fractures are not critically stressed. In order to reach stress criticality we have to assume zero cohesion, as illustrated in one of the key wells (well 6) or increase the pore pressure significantly. Reduction of the cohesion to zero in well 6 would only induce stress criticality in $5 . 5 \\%$ of the fractures in this well. (For interpretation of the references to colour in this figure legend, the reader is referred to the web version of this article.)
